square "pixels".  That makes the ball and paddles move smoothly, and the arena
stays square on screen whatever the terminal's cell shape.  Every wall is tinted
with its owner's colour (dim grey while nobody owns it); empty or eliminated
walls are solid, exactly like in the simulation.

Controls depend on which wall you own:
    left / right wall players (P1, P2):   W / Up = up      S / Down = down
    top / bottom wall players (P3, P4):   A / Left = left  D / Right = right
    Space = stop    Q = quit

Terminals only report key *presses* (never releases), so by default a key moves
your paddle for HOLD seconds; holding the key keeps it moving once your OS key
repeat starts.  In sticky mode a key sets a direction that lasts until Space or
the opposite key - far easier to play, so the menu uses it by default.

Everything that decides *what* to draw (Layout, render_pixels, to_cells) is pure
and unit-tested; the curses calls live in View.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import curses
import locale
import os
import time
from collections import deque
from typing import Deque, List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple

from . import config as C
from . import protocol as P

NAMES = ("left", "right", "top", "bottom")
HOLD = 0.20                  # seconds a single key press keeps the paddle moving
FPS = 30
MIN_COLS, MIN_ROWS = 50, 17  # smallest terminal the arena view works in
MAX_SIDE = 72                # arena side in pixels (36 terminal rows)
PADDLE_PX = 2                # paddle thickness in pixels

# --- logical colours (mapped to terminal colours by Colors) ----------------------
# NOTE: P1..P4 correspond to player slots 0..3 (color ID = slot + 1).
# The other IDs (BALL, WALL, etc.) are internal to this TUI module and independent
# of protocol message constants or game state phase constants.
BG = 0
P1, P2, P3, P4 = 1, 2, 3, 4          # player i uses colour P1 + i
BALL, WALL = 5, 6
TRAIL1, TRAIL2, TRAIL3 = 7, 8, 9     # newest ... oldest ball trail dot
TEXT, DIM, ACCENT, BAD, GOOD, WARN = 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15

PALETTE_256 = {P1: 80, P2: 211, P3: 222, P4: 114, BALL: 231, WALL: 238,
               TRAIL1: 252, TRAIL2: 246, TRAIL3: 240,
               TEXT: 252, DIM: 244, ACCENT: 73, BAD: 203, GOOD: 114, WARN: 221}
PALETTE_8 = {P1: curses.COLOR_CYAN, P2: curses.COLOR_MAGENTA, P3: curses.COLOR_YELLOW,
             P4: curses.COLOR_GREEN, BALL: curses.COLOR_WHITE, WALL: curses.COLOR_BLUE,
             TRAIL1: curses.COLOR_WHITE, TRAIL2: curses.COLOR_BLUE, TRAIL3: curses.COLOR_BLUE,
             TEXT: curses.COLOR_WHITE, DIM: curses.COLOR_BLUE, ACCENT: curses.COLOR_CYAN,
             BAD: curses.COLOR_RED, GOOD: curses.COLOR_GREEN, WARN: curses.COLOR_YELLOW}


# =============================================================================
# keyboard
# =============================================================================

class HumanInput:
    """Callable used as GameClient.input_fn: returns the direction currently held."""

    def __init__(self, sticky: bool = False) -> None:
        self.sticky = sticky
        self._dir = 0
        self._until = 0.0

    def press(self, direction: int) -> None:
        self._dir = direction
        self._until = time.monotonic() + HOLD

    def stop(self) -> None:
        self._dir = 0

    def __call__(self, snap: Optional[P.Snapshot], slot: int) -> int:
        if self.sticky or time.monotonic() < self._until:
            return self._dir
        return 0


def key_to_direction(key: int, slot: int) -> Optional[int]:
    """-1/+1 = move, 0 = stop, None = key not used."""
    if key == ord(" "):
        return 0
    if slot < 2:      # paddle moves up/down
        if key in (curses.KEY_UP, ord("w"), ord("W")):
            return -1
        if key in (curses.KEY_DOWN, ord("s"), ord("S")):
            return 1
    else:             # paddle moves left/right
        if key in (curses.KEY_LEFT, ord("a"), ord("A")):
            return -1
        if key in (curses.KEY_RIGHT, ord("d"), ord("D")):
            return 1
    return None


# =============================================================================
# pure drawing logic
# =============================================================================

class Layout:
    """Where everything goes for a terminal of `rows` x `cols`.

        row 0           title and connection statistics
        row 1           player chips (colour, lives)
        row 2           top of the frame
        rows 3..2+R     arena (R rows of two pixels each = N = 2R pixels square)
        row 3+R         bottom of the frame
        row 5+R ...     key help (a rounded box when there is room, else one line)
    """

    def __init__(self, rows: int, cols: int) -> None:
        self.rows, self.cols = rows, cols
        self.boxed_footer = rows >= 30
        footer_h = 3 if self.boxed_footer else 1
        r = min(rows - (5 + footer_h), (cols - 2) // 2, MAX_SIDE // 2)
        self.R = max(r, 0)
        self.N = 2 * self.R
        self.ok = self.R >= 10 and cols >= MIN_COLS and rows >= MIN_ROWS
        self.x0 = max(0, (cols - (self.N + 2)) // 2)      # frame's left column
        self.top = 2
        self.ay = 3                                       # first arena row
        self.bottom = 3 + self.R
        self.footer_y = 5 + self.R


def alive(snap: Optional[P.Snapshot], i: int) -> bool:
    return snap is not None and bool(snap.active[i]) and snap.lives[i] > 0


def render_pixels(snap: Optional[P.Snapshot], side: int,
                  trail: Sequence[Tuple[float, float]] = (), show_ball: bool = True) -> List[List[int]]:
    """The arena as a side x side grid of logical colour ids (BG = empty)."""
    buf = [[BG] * side for _ in range(side)]

    def fill(x0: int, y0: int, x1: int, y1: int, color: int) -> None:
        for y in range(max(0, y0), min(side - 1, y1) + 1):
            row = buf[y]
            for x in range(max(0, x0), min(side - 1, x1) + 1):
                row[x] = color

    t = PADDLE_PX
    half = C.PADDLE_LEN / 2
    for i in range(C.MAX_PLAYERS):
        if alive(snap, i):
            a = int(round((snap.paddles[i] - half) * side))
            b = max(a, int(round((snap.paddles[i] + half) * side)) - 1)
            color = P1 + i
        else:                                   # nobody here: the wall is solid
            a, b, color = 0, side - 1, WALL
        if i == 0:
            fill(0, a, t - 1, b, color)
        elif i == 1:
            fill(side - t, a, side - 1, b, color)
        elif i == 2:
            fill(a, 0, b, t - 1, color)
        else:
            fill(a, side - t, b, side - 1, color)

    if snap is not None and snap.phase == P.PHASE_PLAYING:
        shades = (TRAIL1, TRAIL2, TRAIL3)
        n = len(trail)
        for j, (tx, ty) in enumerate(trail):    # oldest first
            px = min(max(int(tx * side), 0), side - 1)
            py = min(max(int(ty * side), 0), side - 1)
            if buf[py][px] == BG:
                buf[py][px] = shades[min(n - 1 - j, 2)]
        if show_ball:
            bx = min(max(int(snap.ball_x * side) - 1, 0), side - 2)
            by = min(max(int(snap.ball_y * side) - 1, 0), side - 2)
            fill(bx, by, bx + 1, by + 1, BALL)
    return buf


Run = Tuple[int, str, int, int]      # (cell column, text, foreground colour, background colour)


def to_cells(buf: List[List[int]], unicode_ok: bool = True) -> List[List[Run]]:
    """Pixel grid -> per cell row, runs of identically coloured characters."""
    width = len(buf[0]) if buf else 0
    rows: List[List[Run]] = []
    for cy in range(len(buf) // 2):
        top, bot = buf[2 * cy], buf[2 * cy + 1]
        runs: List[Run] = []
        for x in range(width):
            t, b = top[x], bot[x]
            if t == BG and b == BG:
                continue
            if not unicode_ok:
                ch, fg, bg = "#", (t or b), BG
            elif t == b:
                ch, fg, bg = "█", t, BG          # full block
            elif b == BG:
                ch, fg, bg = "▀", t, BG          # upper half
            elif t == BG:
                ch, fg, bg = "▄", b, BG          # lower half
            else:
                ch, fg, bg = "▀", t, b           # two colours in one cell
            if runs and runs[-1][0] + len(runs[-1][1]) == x and runs[-1][2] == fg and runs[-1][3] == bg:
                runs[-1] = (runs[-1][0], runs[-1][1] + ch, fg, bg)
            else:
                runs.append((x, ch, fg, bg))
        rows.append(runs)
    return rows


class Glyphs:
    """Box-drawing and symbol characters, with a plain-ASCII fallback."""

    def __init__(self, unicode_ok: bool) -> None:
        if unicode_ok:
            self.f_tl, self.f_tr, self.f_bl, self.f_br, self.f_h, self.f_v = "┏", "┓", "┗", "┛", "━", "┃"
            self.b_tl, self.b_tr, self.b_bl, self.b_br, self.b_h, self.b_v = "╭", "╮", "╰", "╯", "─", "│"
            self.heart, self.lost, self.chip, self.chip_off = "♥", "·", "■", "□"
            self.up, self.down, self.left, self.right = "↑", "↓", "←", "→"
            self.sep, self.mark_l, self.mark_r = "·", "▸", "◂"
        else:
            self.f_tl = self.f_tr = self.f_bl = self.f_br = "+"
            self.b_tl = self.b_tr = self.b_bl = self.b_br = "+"
            self.f_h = self.b_h = "-"
            self.f_v = self.b_v = "|"
            self.heart, self.lost, self.chip, self.chip_off = "*", ".", "#", "o"
            self.up, self.down, self.left, self.right = "Up", "Down", "Left", "Right"
            self.sep, self.mark_l, self.mark_r = "|", ">", "<"


def want_unicode() -> bool:
    """True when the terminal library can really draw Unicode.

    Ask the C library's codeset, not Python's: in a plain C/POSIX locale Python still
    reports UTF-8 (its own UTF-8 mode), but curses would silently drop every non-ASCII
    character and the arena would vanish.
    """
    if os.environ.get("PONG_ASCII"):
        return False
    try:
        codeset = locale.nl_langinfo(locale.CODESET)
    except (AttributeError, ValueError):
        codeset = locale.getpreferredencoding(False)
    return "utf" in (codeset or "").lower()


def key_help(slot: int, sticky: bool, is_bot: bool, out: bool, g: Glyphs) -> str:
    if out:
        return "you're out - watching the rest of the game  %s  Q quit" % g.sep
    if is_bot:
        return "a bot is playing for you  %s  Q quit" % g.sep
    if slot < 2:
        keys = "W/S or %s/%s" % (g.up, g.down)
    else:
        keys = "A/D or %s/%s" % (g.left, g.right)
    verb = "steer" if sticky else "move"
    return "%s %s  %s  Space stop  %s  Q quit" % (keys, verb, g.sep, g.sep)


# =============================================================================
# curses plumbing
# =============================================================================

class Colors:
    """Allocates curses colour pairs on demand for (foreground, background) logical colours."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.enabled = False
        self._pairs = {}
        self._next = 1
        self._palette = PALETTE_8
        self._default_bg = -1

    def init(self) -> None:
        if not curses.has_colors():
            return
        curses.start_color()
        try:
            curses.use_default_colors()
        except curses.error:
            self._default_bg = curses.COLOR_BLACK
        self.enabled = True
        if curses.COLORS >= 256:
            self._palette = PALETTE_256

    def attr(self, fg: int, bg: int = BG, bold: bool = False, dim: bool = False) -> int:
        a = (curses.A_BOLD if bold else 0) | (curses.A_DIM if dim else 0)
        if not self.enabled:
            return a
        key = (fg, bg)
        pair = self._pairs.get(key)
        if pair is None:
            if self._next >= curses.COLOR_PAIRS:
                return a
            pair = self._next
            self._next += 1
            curses.init_pair(pair, self._palette.get(fg, curses.COLOR_WHITE),
                             self._default_bg if bg == BG else self._palette.get(bg, self._default_bg))
            self._pairs[key] = pair
        return a | curses.color_pair(pair)


def put(scr, y: int, x: int, text: str, attr: int = 0) -> None:
    """addstr that clips instead of raising at the edges."""
    rows, cols = scr.getmaxyx()
    if y < 0 or y >= rows or x >= cols or not text:
        return
    if x < 0:
        text, x = text[-x:], 0
    text = text[:cols - x]
    try:
        scr.addstr(y, x, text, attr)
    except (curses.error, UnicodeError):
        pass


class View:
    """Draws the live game from a GameClient's latest snapshot."""

    def __init__(self, scr, client, human: Optional[HumanInput], ascii_mode: Optional[bool] = None) -> None:
        self.scr = scr
        self.client = client
        self.human = human
        self.unicode = want_unicode() if ascii_mode is None else not ascii_mode
        self.g = Glyphs(self.unicode)
        self.colors = Colors()
        self.colors.init()
        self.trail: Deque[Tuple[float, float]] = deque(maxlen=3)
        self._last_tick: Optional[int] = None
        self._last_ball: Optional[Tuple[float, float]] = None
        self._ticks = 0
        self._still = 0
        self._frame = 0

    # --- state that needs history ---------------------------------------------------

    def _update_history(self, snap: Optional[P.Snapshot]) -> None:
        if snap is None or snap.tick == self._last_tick:
            return
        self._last_tick = snap.tick
        ball = (snap.ball_x, snap.ball_y)
        if snap.phase != P.PHASE_PLAYING:
            self.trail.clear()
            self._still = 0
        else:
            if self._last_ball is not None:
                dx, dy = ball[0] - self._last_ball[0], ball[1] - self._last_ball[1]
                if dx * dx + dy * dy > 0.04:            # re-serve: don't draw a streak across the arena
                    self.trail.clear()
                self._still = self._still + 1 if (dx == 0 and dy == 0) else 0
            self._ticks += 1
            if self._ticks % 3 == 0:
                self.trail.append(ball)
        self._last_ball = ball

    # --- one frame ----------------------------------------------------------------------

    def draw(self) -> None:
        scr, colors, g = self.scr, self.colors, self.g
        scr.erase()
        rows, cols = scr.getmaxyx()
        lay = Layout(rows, cols)
        if not lay.ok:
            msg = "Window too small (need %dx%d)" % (MIN_COLS, MIN_ROWS)
            put(scr, rows // 2, max(0, (cols - len(msg)) // 2), msg, colors.attr(BAD, bold=True))
            scr.refresh()
            return

        snap = self.client.get_snapshot()
        me = self.client.player_id
        self._update_history(snap)
        self._frame += 1

        self._draw_header(lay, snap, me)
        self._draw_chips(lay, snap, me)
        self._draw_frame(lay, snap, me)
        self._draw_arena(lay, snap)
        self._draw_banner(lay, snap, me)
        self._draw_footer(lay, snap, me)
        scr.refresh()

    def _draw_header(self, lay: Layout, snap, me: int) -> None:
        scr, colors = self.scr, self.colors
        title = "4-PLAYER PONG"
        for i, ch in enumerate(title):
            put(scr, 0, 2 + i, ch, colors.attr(P1 + i % 4, bold=True))

        st = self.client.stats()
        rtt = "ping %.1f ms" % (1000 * st["rtt"]) if st["rtt"] is not None else "ping -- ms"
        loss_pct = 100 * st["loss_ratio"]
        loss = "loss %.1f%%" % loss_pct
        loss_col = GOOD if loss_pct < 1 else (WARN if loss_pct < 5 else BAD)
        x = lay.cols - 2 - len(rtt) - 3 - len(loss)
        if x > 2 + len(title) + 2:
            put(scr, 0, x, rtt, colors.attr(TEXT))
            put(scr, 0, x + len(rtt) + 3, loss, colors.attr(loss_col, bold=True))
            who = "you are P%d (%s wall)" % (me + 1, NAMES[me])
            wx = 2 + len(title) + 3
            if wx + len(who) < x - 1:
                put(scr, 0, wx, who, colors.attr(DIM))

    def _draw_chips(self, lay: Layout, snap, me: int) -> None:
        scr, colors, g = self.scr, self.colors, self.g
        chips: List[List[Tuple[str, int]]] = []
        for i in range(C.MAX_PLAYERS):
            col = P1 + i
            if snap is None or not snap.active[i]:
                chips.append([(g.chip_off + " P%d --" % (i + 1), colors.attr(DIM))])
            elif snap.lives[i] <= 0:
                chips.append([(g.chip + " P%d OUT" % (i + 1), colors.attr(DIM))])
            else:
                lives = snap.lives[i]
                parts = [(g.chip, colors.attr(col)),
                         (" P%d " % (i + 1), colors.attr(TEXT, bold=(i == me))),
                         (g.heart * lives, colors.attr(col))]
                if lives < C.LIVES:
                    parts.append((g.lost * (C.LIVES - lives), colors.attr(DIM)))
                if i == me:
                    parts.append((" (you)", colors.attr(TEXT, bold=True)))
                chips.append(parts)
        gap = "   "
        total = sum(sum(len(t) for t, _ in c) for c in chips) + len(gap) * (len(chips) - 1)
        x = max(0, (lay.cols - total) // 2)
        for c in chips:
            for text, attr in c:
                put(scr, 1, x, text, attr)
                x += len(text)
            x += len(gap)

    def _draw_frame(self, lay: Layout, snap, me: int) -> None:
        scr, colors, g = self.scr, self.colors, self.g

        def side(i: int) -> int:
            if alive(snap, i):
                return colors.attr(P1 + i, bold=(i == me))
            return colors.attr(WALL)

        corner = colors.attr(ACCENT)
        right = lay.x0 + 1 + lay.N
        put(scr, lay.top, lay.x0, g.f_tl, corner)
        put(scr, lay.top, lay.x0 + 1, g.f_h * lay.N, side(2))
        put(scr, lay.top, right, g.f_tr, corner)
        for r in range(lay.R):
            put(scr, lay.ay + r, lay.x0, g.f_v, side(0))
            put(scr, lay.ay + r, right, g.f_v, side(1))
        put(scr, lay.bottom, lay.x0, g.f_bl, corner)
        put(scr, lay.bottom, lay.x0 + 1, g.f_h * lay.N, side(3))
        put(scr, lay.bottom, right, g.f_br, corner)

    def _draw_arena(self, lay: Layout, snap) -> None:
        # while the ball waits to be served it blinks, so you can see the game is about to start
        blink_off = self._still >= 2 and (self._frame // 8) % 2 == 1
        buf = render_pixels(snap, lay.N, self.trail, show_ball=not blink_off)
        for cy, runs in enumerate(to_cells(buf, self.unicode)):
            for x, text, fg, bg in runs:
                put(self.scr, lay.ay + cy, lay.x0 + 1 + x, text, self.colors.attr(fg, bg))

    def _banner_lines(self, snap, me: int) -> Optional[List[Tuple[str, int, bool]]]:
        """(text, colour, bold) lines for the box in the middle of the arena, or None."""
        if snap is None:
            return [("CONNECTING", TEXT, True), ("waiting for the server", DIM, False)]
        quiet = self.client.seconds_since_data()
        if quiet > 2.0:
            return [("NO DATA FROM SERVER", BAD, True), ("%.0f s without an update" % quiet, DIM, False)]
        if snap.phase == P.PHASE_WAITING:
            return [("WAITING FOR PLAYERS", TEXT, True),
                    ("%d of 2 needed have joined" % sum(snap.active), DIM, False)]
        if snap.phase == P.PHASE_GAME_OVER:
            winners = [i for i in range(C.MAX_PLAYERS) if alive(snap, i)]
            if len(winners) == 1:
                w = winners[0]
                head = ("YOU WIN!", P1 + w, True) if w == me else ("P%d WINS" % (w + 1), P1 + w, True)
            else:
                head = ("DRAW", TEXT, True)
            return [head, ("next game starting...", DIM, False)]
        return None

    def _draw_banner(self, lay: Layout, snap, me: int) -> None:
        lines = self._banner_lines(snap, me)
        if not lines:
            return
        scr, colors, g = self.scr, self.colors, self.g
        w = max(len(t) for t, _, _ in lines) + 6
        h = len(lines) + 2
        x = lay.x0 + 1 + lay.N // 2 - w // 2
        y = lay.ay + lay.R // 2 - h // 2
        edge = colors.attr(ACCENT)
        put(scr, y, x, g.b_tl + g.b_h * (w - 2) + g.b_tr, edge)
        for i, (text, col, bold) in enumerate(lines):
            put(scr, y + 1 + i, x, g.b_v + " " * (w - 2) + g.b_v, edge)
            put(scr, y + 1 + i, x + (w - len(text)) // 2, text, colors.attr(col, bold=bold))
        put(scr, y + h - 1, x, g.b_bl + g.b_h * (w - 2) + g.b_br, edge)

    def _draw_footer(self, lay: Layout, snap, me: int) -> None:
        scr, colors, g = self.scr, self.colors, self.g
        out = snap is not None and snap.active[me] and snap.lives[me] <= 0
        text = key_help(me, bool(self.human and self.human.sticky), self.human is None, out, g)
        attr = colors.attr(DIM)
        if lay.boxed_footer:
            w = max(lay.N + 2, len(text) + 4)
            x = max(0, (lay.cols - w) // 2)
            put(scr, lay.footer_y, x, g.b_tl + g.b_h * (w - 2) + g.b_tr, attr)
            put(scr, lay.footer_y + 1, x, g.b_v, attr)
            put(scr, lay.footer_y + 1, x + w - 1, g.b_v, attr)
            put(scr, lay.footer_y + 1, x + (w - len(text)) // 2, text, colors.attr(TEXT))
            put(scr, lay.footer_y + 2, x, g.b_bl + g.b_h * (w - 2) + g.b_br, attr)
        else:
            put(scr, lay.footer_y, max(0, (lay.cols - len(text)) // 2), text, attr)


# =============================================================================
# entry point used by game.client and the menu
# =============================================================================

def run(client, human: Optional[HumanInput], duration: float = 0.0, ascii_mode: Optional[bool] = None) -> None:
    """Show the game until the player quits (or `duration` seconds pass)."""
    try:
        locale.setlocale(locale.LC_ALL, "")
    except locale.Error:
        pass
    os.environ.setdefault("ESCDELAY", "25")
    curses.wrapper(_main, client, human, duration, ascii_mode)


def _main(scr, client, human, duration: float, ascii_mode: Optional[bool]) -> None:
    curses.curs_set(0)
    scr.nodelay(True)
    scr.keypad(True)
    view = View(scr, client, human, ascii_mode)
    slot = client.player_id
    end = time.monotonic() + duration if duration else None
    while True:
        while True:
            ch = scr.getch()
            if ch == -1:
                break
            if ch in (ord("q"), ord("Q")):          # not Esc: a terminal that mis-reports arrow keys sends ESC [ ...
                return
            if human is not None:
                d = key_to_direction(ch, slot)
                if d == 0:
                    human.stop()
                elif d is not None:
                    human.press(d)
        if end is not None and time.monotonic() >= end:
            return
        view.draw()
        time.sleep(1.0 / FPS)

=== FOLDER: /game ===
=== FILE: __main__.py ===
import sys

from .menu import main

sys.exit(main())

=== FOLDER: /game ===
=== FILE: state.py ===
"""The 4-player pong simulation.  Pure logic: no sockets, no threads, no clock.

Arena = unit square.  Slot 0 owns the LEFT wall, 1 the RIGHT, 2 the TOP, 3 the
BOTTOM.  Slots 0/1 move their paddle along y, slots 2/3 along x.  Input -1 moves
the paddle toward 0 (up / left), +1 toward 1 (down / right).

An empty or eliminated slot's wall is solid: the ball just bounces off it.
A live player whose paddle misses loses a life and the ball is re-served from
the centre.  The game ends when one or zero players are alive, pauses for
GAME_OVER_DELAY, then restarts automatically (so traffic never stops during
measurements).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import math
import random
from typing import List, Optional

from . import config as C
from .protocol import PHASE_GAME_OVER, PHASE_PLAYING, PHASE_WAITING, Snapshot


class GameState:
    def __init__(self, seed: Optional[int] = None) -> None:
        self.rng = random.Random(seed)
        self.tick = 0
        self.phase = PHASE_WAITING
        self.active: List[bool] = [False] * C.MAX_PLAYERS
        self.lives: List[int] = [C.LIVES] * C.MAX_PLAYERS
        self.paddles: List[float] = [0.5] * C.MAX_PLAYERS
        self.inputs: List[int] = [0] * C.MAX_PLAYERS
        self.ball_x = 0.5
        self.ball_y = 0.5
        self.vx = 0.0
        self.vy = 0.0
        self.speed = C.BALL_SPEED
        self.serve_timer = 0.0
        self.over_timer = 0.0
        self.winner: Optional[int] = None

    # --- player management -------------------------------------------------

    def set_active(self, slot: int, flag: bool) -> None:
        self.active[slot] = flag
        self.inputs[slot] = 0
        if flag:
            self.lives[slot] = C.LIVES
            self.paddles[slot] = 0.5

    def set_input(self, slot: int, direction: int) -> None:
        self.inputs[slot] = max(-1, min(1, int(direction)))

    # --- simulation ----------------------------------------------------------

    def step(self, dt: float) -> None:
        self.tick += 1
        self._move_paddles(dt)
        n_active = sum(self.active)

        if self.phase == PHASE_WAITING:
            if n_active >= 2:
                self._new_game()
            return

        if self.phase == PHASE_GAME_OVER:
            self.over_timer -= dt
            if self.over_timer <= 0:
                if n_active >= 2:
                    self._new_game()
                else:
                    self.phase = PHASE_WAITING
                    self._park_ball()
            return

        alive = self._alive()
        if len(alive) <= 1:
            self.phase = PHASE_GAME_OVER
            self.winner = alive[0] if alive else None
            self.over_timer = C.GAME_OVER_DELAY
            self._park_ball()
            return

        if self.serve_timer > 0:
            self.serve_timer -= dt
            return
        self._move_ball(dt)

    def _alive(self) -> List[int]:
        return [i for i in range(C.MAX_PLAYERS) if self.active[i] and self.lives[i] > 0]

    def _move_paddles(self, dt: float) -> None:
        lo = C.PADDLE_LEN / 2
        hi = 1.0 - lo
        for i in range(C.MAX_PLAYERS):
            if self.inputs[i]:
                self.paddles[i] = min(hi, max(lo, self.paddles[i] + self.inputs[i] * C.PADDLE_SPEED * dt))

    def _new_game(self) -> None:
        for i in range(C.MAX_PLAYERS):
            if self.active[i]:
                self.lives[i] = C.LIVES
        self.winner = None
        self.phase = PHASE_PLAYING
        self._serve()

    def _park_ball(self) -> None:
        self.ball_x = self.ball_y = 0.5
        self.vx = self.vy = 0.0

    def _serve(self) -> None:
        self.ball_x = self.ball_y = 0.5
        self.speed = C.BALL_SPEED
        angle = math.radians(self.rng.choice((45, 135, 225, 315)) + self.rng.uniform(-25, 25))
        self.vx = math.cos(angle) * self.speed
        self.vy = math.sin(angle) * self.speed
        self.serve_timer = C.SERVE_DELAY

    def _move_ball(self, dt: float) -> None:
        self.ball_x += self.vx * dt
        self.ball_y += self.vy * dt
        r = C.BALL_RADIUS
        if self.ball_x - r <= 0 and self.vx < 0:
            self._wall_hit(0, self.ball_y)
            return
        if self.ball_x + r >= 1 and self.vx > 0:
            self._wall_hit(1, self.ball_y)
            return
        if self.ball_y - r <= 0 and self.vy < 0:
            self._wall_hit(2, self.ball_x)
            return
        if self.ball_y + r >= 1 and self.vy > 0:
            self._wall_hit(3, self.ball_x)
            return

    def _wall_hit(self, slot: int, coord: float) -> bool:
        """Ball reached `slot`'s wall at position `coord` along it.

        Returns True if the ball was lost and re-served (stop processing walls).
        """
        alive = self.active[slot] and self.lives[slot] > 0
        reach = C.PADDLE_LEN / 2 + C.BALL_RADIUS
        if alive and abs(coord - self.paddles[slot]) > reach:
            self.lives[slot] -= 1
            self._serve()
            return True

        spin = 0.0
        if alive:  # paddle hit: speed up and add spin from where it hit the paddle
            offset = (coord - self.paddles[slot]) / (C.PADDLE_LEN / 2)
            offset = max(-1.0, min(1.0, offset))
            self.speed = min(self.speed * C.BALL_SPEEDUP, C.BALL_MAX_SPEED)
            spin = offset * 0.5 * self.speed

        s = self.speed
        r = C.BALL_RADIUS
        if slot < 2:  # left / right wall: flip vx, spin changes vy
            sign = 1.0 if slot == 0 else -1.0
            vt = max(-0.85 * s, min(0.85 * s, self.vy + spin))
            self.vx = sign * math.sqrt(s * s - vt * vt)
            self.vy = vt
            self.ball_x = r if slot == 0 else 1.0 - r
        else:         # top / bottom wall: flip vy, spin changes vx
            sign = 1.0 if slot == 2 else -1.0
            vt = max(-0.85 * s, min(0.85 * s, self.vx + spin))
            self.vy = sign * math.sqrt(s * s - vt * vt)
            self.vx = vt
            self.ball_y = r if slot == 2 else 1.0 - r
        return False

    # --- output --------------------------------------------------------------

    def snapshot(self) -> Snapshot:
        return Snapshot(
            tick=self.tick,
            phase=self.phase,
            ball_x=self.ball_x,
            ball_y=self.ball_y,
            paddles=tuple(self.paddles),
            lives=tuple(self.lives),
            active=tuple(int(a) for a in self.active),
        )

=== FOLDER: /game ===
=== FILE: seq.py ===
"""Sequence-number tracking: the heart of the packet-loss handling."""
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Optional, Tuple

NEW = "new"              # higher than anything seen so far -> use it
DUPLICATE = "duplicate"  # already received -> ignore
LATE = "late"            # arrived out of order, fills an earlier gap -> ignore for state
STALE = "stale"          # too old to classify -> ignore


class SeqTracker:
    """Classifies the sequence numbers of ONE stream of packets.

    A stream is one sender sending one kind of message with its own counter
    (e.g. the server's STATE_BROADCASTs, or one client's STATE_UPDATEs).

    ``observe(seq)`` returns ``(kind, gap)``:
      * NEW        gap = how many sequence numbers were skipped just before it
      * DUPLICATE / LATE / STALE   gap = 0

    Only NEW packets should change game state ("use the latest state").  Loss is
    counted exactly: ``lost = expected - received`` where late packets that fill
    a gap are counted as received after all.  Packets before the first one we
    ever saw are not counted (a client can join mid-stream).
    """

    WINDOW = 64  # how far back we remember which sequence numbers arrived

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.reset()

    def reset(self) -> None:
        self.first: Optional[int] = None
        self.highest: Optional[int] = None
        self._mask = 0          # bit i set  <=>  (highest - i) was received
        self.received = 0       # unique packets received (new + late)
        self.duplicates = 0
        self.late = 0
        self.stale = 0

    def observe(self, seq: int) -> Tuple[str, int]:
        full = (1 << self.WINDOW) - 1
        if self.highest is None:
            self.first = self.highest = seq
            self._mask = 1
            self.received = 1
            return NEW, 0

        # Sanity bound: reject absurd sequence jumps (e.g. > 100,000) to prevent packet-poisoning / metrics corruption
        if seq > self.highest and seq - self.highest > 100000:
            self.stale += 1
            return STALE, 0

        if seq > self.highest:
            shift = seq - self.highest
            self._mask = 1 if shift >= self.WINDOW else ((self._mask << shift) | 1) & full
            self.highest = seq
            self.received += 1
            return NEW, shift - 1

        delta = self.highest - seq
        if delta >= self.WINDOW:
            self.stale += 1
            return STALE, 0
        bit = 1 << delta
        if self._mask & bit:
            self.duplicates += 1
            return DUPLICATE, 0
        self._mask |= bit
        self.received += 1
        self.late += 1
        return LATE, 0

    @property
    def expected(self) -> int:
        if self.first is None or self.highest is None:
            return 0
        return self.highest - self.first + 1

    @property
    def lost(self) -> int:
        return max(0, self.expected - self.received)

    @property
    def loss_ratio(self) -> float:
        return self.lost / self.expected if self.expected else 0.0

=== FOLDER: /. ===
=== FILE: README.md ===
# 4-player Pong over UDP (Team 11)

Standard library only. Run everything from this folder (`pong/`).

## Run the tests
    python3 -m unittest discover -v

## Play on one machine
    python3 -m game.server                       # terminal 1
    python3 -m game.client --bot                 # terminal 2: bot (shows the TUI)
    python3 -m game.client --bot                 # terminal 3: another bot
    python3 -m game.client                       # terminal 4: YOU (keyboard)

Controls: left/right-wall players use W/S (or Up/Down); top/bottom-wall players use
A/D (or Left/Right). Space stops, Q quits. `--sticky` makes a key press persist.
Any mix of humans and bots works; a game starts as soon as 2 players are in.

## Experiment mode (no UI)
    python3 -m game.server --log results/raw/server.csv
    python3 -m game.client --server <ip> --bot --headless --duration 60 --log results/raw/h1.csv

`GAME_DROP_PROB=0.1` (or `--drop 0.1`) makes a process discard 10% of received packets,
for testing loss handling without Mininet.

## Mininet (run in the Ubuntu VM, as root)
    sudo apt install -y ethtool                              # once
    sudo python3 -B topo/topology.py --smoke 15              # automated check of the whole setup
    sudo python3 -B topo/topology.py --cli --bots 3          # 3 bots + server; you join from another tab
    python3 -m analysis.metrics results/raw/smoke/*.csv      # latency / jitter / loss table

Topology: h1-h4 (players) and hb1 (background source) on s1; hs (server) and hb2 (background
sink) on s2; s1<->s2 is the 10 Mbit/s bottleneck, shaped in both directions by an OVS QoS.
`--qos plain` is the baseline FIFO, `--qos priority` adds queue 1 (game) and 2 (background),
`--controller remote` waits for a Ryu controller on 127.0.0.1:6653.

## SDN controllers (Ryu, Python 3.9 environment in ~/ryu-env)
    # terminal 1: the controller
    cd ~/pes_projects/CN_project/pong
    PYTHONPATH=. ~/ryu-env/bin/ryu-manager sdn/baseline_switch.py     # or sdn/qos_switch.py
    # terminal 2: the experiment (same controller must be running)
    sudo python3 -B topo/topology.py --smoke 15 --bg-mbps 20 --controller remote --qos plain      # baseline
    sudo python3 -B topo/topology.py --smoke 15 --bg-mbps 20 --controller remote --qos priority   # with qos_switch.py

`baseline_switch.py` is a plain L2 learning switch; `qos_switch.py` subclasses it and only adds
"UDP port 5000 -> queue 1, everything else -> queue 2".  Do not run `sudo mn -c` while Ryu is
running: it kills ryu-manager.

## Layout
    game/config.py    constants shared with Mininet + Ryu (UDP port 5000, tick rate, ...)
    game/protocol.py  wire format
    game/seq.py       sequence tracker (loss / duplicate / late detection)
    game/state.py     game simulation (pure logic)
    game/server.py    UDP server (receive thread + tick thread)
    game/client.py    UDP client (human or bot)
    game/bot.py       computer player
    game/tui.py       curses UI + keyboard input
    game/signals.py   SIGTERM == Ctrl-C so experiment scripts can stop processes cleanly
    sdn/baseline_switch.py  baseline controller (learning switch)
    sdn/qos_switch.py       priority controller (game -> queue 1)
    topo/topology.py  Mininet network, bottleneck QoS, smoke test, demo mode
    analysis/metrics.py  latency / jitter / loss from the CSV logs
    bundle.py         utility to package the repository into a single text dump

# First comparison: plain switch vs SDN priority

Setup: 4 bot players, 15 s run, 10 Mbit/s bottleneck, 20 Mbit/s UDP background traffic in each direction.
Baseline = `sdn/baseline_switch.py` + `--qos plain`. Priority = `sdn/qos_switch.py` + `--qos priority`.
One run each, averaged over the 4 players. The first 1.5 s of each run had no background traffic.

| Metric | Baseline | Priority | Improvement |
|---|---|---|---|
| One-way delay, mean | 73.9 ms | 0.13 ms | ~560x lower |
| One-way delay, p95 | 109.5 ms | 0.25 ms | ~440x lower |
| Jitter | 23.9 ms | 0.05 ms | ~470x lower |
| Round-trip (PING/PONG) | 139 ms | 0.17 ms | ~810x lower |
| Packet loss | 0% | 0% | none |

Queue counters (priority run): ~1,870 game packets in queue 1 and ~15,000 background packets in queue 2 on each bottleneck port.

=== FOLDER: /scripts ===
=== FILE: clean_results.py ===
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Clean up old experiment results.

Deletes raw CSV files older than --keep days, or removes all results if --all.
Note: --keep 0 (the default) disables time-based deletion entirely, making the
script a safe no-op unless --all or an explicit --keep N > 0 is provided.

    python3 scripts/clean_results.py --keep 7   # keep last 7 days
    python3 scripts/clean_results.py --all       # delete everything
    python3 scripts/clean_results.py --dry-run   # show what would be deleted
"""
import argparse
import os
import shutil
import time


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    ap.add_argument("--results-dir", default="results", help="results folder to clean")
    ap.add_argument("--keep", type=int, default=0,
                    help="keep only raw/ files newer than N days (default: 0 = disabled / no-op)")
    ap.add_argument("--all", action="store_true", help="delete ALL results (raw/, summary.csv, graphs/)")
    ap.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true", help="show what would be deleted")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    base = args.results_dir
    if not os.path.isdir(base):
        print("No results directory at", base)
        return 0

    deleted = []
    now = time.time()
    keep_seconds = args.keep * 86400

    # Raw CSV folders
    raw_dir = os.path.join(base, "raw")
    if os.path.isdir(raw_dir):
        for name in os.listdir(raw_dir):
            path = os.path.join(raw_dir, name)
            if not os.path.isdir(path):
                continue
            mtime = os.path.getmtime(path)
            if args.all or (keep_seconds and now - mtime > keep_seconds):
                deleted.append(path)
                if not args.dry_run:
                    shutil.rmtree(path)

    # Summary CSV
    summary = os.path.join(base, "summary.csv")
    if args.all and os.path.exists(summary):
        deleted.append(summary)
        if not args.dry_run:
            os.remove(summary)

    # Run state
    state = os.path.join(base, "run_state.json")
    if args.all and os.path.exists(state):
        deleted.append(state)
        if not args.dry_run:
            os.remove(state)

    # Graphs
    graphs = os.path.join(base, "graphs")
    if args.all and os.path.isdir(graphs):
        deleted.append(graphs + "/")
        if not args.dry_run:
            shutil.rmtree(graphs)

    if not deleted:
        print("Nothing to clean")
        return 0

    print("Would delete:" if args.dry_run else "Deleted:")
    for d in deleted:
        print(" ", d)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import sys
    sys.exit(main())
